#### p03 — Mohebbian MR, Vedaei SS, Wahid KA, Dinh A, Marateb HR, Tavakolian K. "Fetal ECG Extraction From Maternal ECG Using Attention-Based CycleGAN." DOI 10.1109/JBHI.2021.3111873. Version read =...

**Mức truy cập:** `full_text_read` · **Tải về:** có (`p03_Mohebbian2022-AttCycleGAN.pdf`, 745,741 byte) · **Độ tin cậy trích xuất:** high

**A. Problem.** Extracting the fetal ECG from a maternal abdominal ECG recording is a blind source separation problem made hard by three things the authors name explicitly: the fetal signal has very low amplitude relative to the maternal one, the maternal and fetal R waves overlap in time, and the abdominal recording is contaminated by baseline drift, motion artefact, EMG, power-line and electrode noise. Classical remedies each fail in a characteristic way: adaptive filters (LMS, RLS) assume a flat/white power spectrum and a morphologically similar reference channel, and degrade under coloured noise; template subtraction and Kalman-type methods break when maternal and fetal QRS coincide; PCA/ICA/periodic component analysis assume a linear stationary mixing matrix and need many abdominal channels, specific electrode placement and heavy post-processing, and fail at low SNR. The authors add a fourth, ML-specific observation that motivates their design: a plain convolutional kernel that learns to amplify the fetal QRS will also amplify the maternal QRS, because the two are highly correlated in the abdominal mixture - so convolution alone cannot suppress the mother. Finally, most prior work extracts only the fetal QRS, whereas P, T and ST morphology are needed for richer analysis; and a model that generalises across subjects with different electrode placements has not been demonstrated.

**B. Data.** Four data sources. (1) PhysioNet Abdominal and Direct FECG (A&D FECG) - the only source with real continuous fetal ground truth: 5 pregnant women (subject 1 = r01, 2 = r07, 3 = r10, 4 = r04, 5 = r08), 38-41 weeks gestation, 5 minutes each, 4 abdominal channels plus a direct fetal scalp electrode, 1000 Hz, 16-bit, band-pass filtered 0-100 Hz at acquisition with digital power-line removal. Electrode montage: four electrodes around the navel, a reference above the pubic symphysis, common reference on the left leg, fixed across all recordings. Ground truth = the scalp FECG waveform itself, which the authors acknowledge (citing Nurani et al.) is itself contaminated by maternal ECG, more so near delivery due to uterine contractions. (2) PhysioNet Non-Invasive FECG (NI-FECG): 55 multichannel abdominal recordings, 21-40 weeks, 1000 Hz, 16-bit, 0-100 Hz; electrode positions are NOT fixed and were moved to improve SNR. 14 sets used (154, 192, 244, 274, 290, 323, 368, 444, 597, 733, 746, 811, 826, 906), chosen to match prior work for comparability. No scalp FECG - only fetal QRS time stamps. (3) PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2013 Set-A: 75 abdominal recordings, 1000 Hz, 4 channels, with reference fetal R-peaks; records a33, a38, a52, a54, a71, a74 excluded for incomplete annotation, leaving 69. (4) Synthetic: FECGSYN toolbox, 24 simulated maternal+fetal ECG pairs spanning fetal HR 115, 125, 135, 145, 155, 160 bpm and maternal HR 65, 77, 89, 100 bpm, used only to probe heart-rate...

**C. Method.** An attention-based CycleGAN that learns an unpaired mapping between the abdominal MECG domain X = {x1..xN} (N x M) and the FECG domain Y = {y1..yP} (P x M). Two generators G: X -> Y and F: Y -> X, and two discriminators Dx (separates X from F(Y)) and Dy (separates Y from G(X)). The objective combines two log-form adversarial losses with a cycle-consistency term weighted by lambda = 4, so the network can be trained without one-to-one paired MECG/FECG examples - the pairing constraint is replaced by the two-step round trip x -> G(x) -> F(G(x)) ~ x. The authors argue this is why the method can be trained on a very small dataset. Three design choices carry the novelty. First, the attention layer is repurposed as an explicit filtering MASK rather than a softmax-weighted context: because convolution kernels that amplify the fetal QRS also amplify the maternal QRS, the attention output is instead z-score normalised and hard-clipped to the interval [0.8, 1], and this mask is element-wise multiplied with the input window, so low-attention (maternal-dominated) regions are attenuated toward 0.8 and the region of interest passes at 1. Second, sine activations replace LeakyReLU everywhere, motivated by SIREN-style implicit representation results showing sinusoids retain fine detail better when translating between signal domains. Third, log(cosh) is used as the reconstruction distance: L2 over-smooths (and, because ECG energy is concentrated at low frequency, can collapse toward constant...

**D. Model.** GENERATOR (Fig. 1, bottom-left). Input path: abdominal MECG -> band-pass -> Savitzky-Golay -> windowing + normalisation, producing a 1 x 200 vector. Attention branch off that vector: Conv1D(number of filters = 1, kernel = 31) -> sine -> MultiHead self-attention with 2 heads and embedding dimension equal to the window size (200) -> z-score normalisation -> clip to [0.8, 1] -> element-wise multiplication back onto the windowed input. Main convolutional stack, four Conv1D layers each followed by a sine activation, in figure order: Conv1D(filters = 13, kernel = 3), Conv1D(filters = 7, kernel = 5), Conv1D(filters = 5, kernel = 13), Conv1D(filters = 1, kernel = 3); the output passes through a Savitzky-Golay filter to give the scalp FECG estimate. The stated design rule is that a larger kernel is used where the signal needs smoothing and a smaller kernel where detail must be retrieved. DISCRIMINATOR (Fig. 1, bottom-right): Conv1D(filters = 12, kernel = 3) -> sine -> Conv1D(filters = 7, kernel = 3) -> sine -> Conv1D(filters = 3, kernel = 3) -> sine -> Conv1D(filters = 1, kernel = 3) -> sine -> Dense(32) -> softmax for real/fake. INPUT/OUTPUT SHAPES: input 1 x 200 samples at 200 Hz = 1.0 s of one abdominal channel, z-scored; output 1 x 200 fetal ECG waveform. TRAINING: 50 epochs, Nesterov Adam optimiser, log(cosh) loss, lambda = 4. Total training time 10400 s at 208 s/epoch on Core-i9, 16 GB RAM, NVIDIA GTX 1060 6 GB; inference 0.14 s per 1000 samples. NOT REPORTED: batch size,...

**E. Evaluation.** Three distinct protocols, with different leakage exposure. (1) A&D FECG signal-extraction and QRS results: explicit leave-one-subject-out cross-validation - "the entire signals except for one subject are used for the train, and the remaining signal is used for tests", repeated 5 times, one per subject. Because each subject contributes exactly one record, LOSO here is also record-wise. LEAKAGE RISK: LOW. Caveat: only 5 subjects, all at 38-41 weeks, so the variance estimate is thin and the gestational range is narrow. (2) Cross-dataset external validation: a single model trained on ALL of A&D FECG and tested on 14 NI-FECG records and 69 CinC 2013 Set-A records. LEAKAGE RISK: LOW - this is the cleanest evidence in the paper, and it is the number to compare against (F1 97.9 and 94.7, versus 99.7 in-dataset). (3) Within-dataset results on NI-FECG and the Challenge: "4-fold cross-validation is performed on the NI-FECG dataset and NI-FECG challenge datasets", with no statement of whether folds are formed over RECORDS or over 1-second WINDOWS. LEAKAGE RISK: UNCLEAR, and potentially HIGH. If the 4 folds are random splits of the 1-second windows drawn from 14 (or 69) records, then windows from the same record, the same subject and the same electrode montage appear in both train and test, which is exactly the high-risk pattern; with 14 records and 4 folds, record-wise grouping is possible but is never asserted. Two further concerns specific to this paper. First, a target-construction...

**F. Results.** SIGNAL EXTRACTION on A&D FECG under LOSO (Table I, subjects 1-5): ICC 0.99, 0.99, 0.98, 0.99, 0.99; R-squared 0.99, 0.98, 0.96, 0.98, 0.98 (abstract summarises as average 98%, CI 95% 97-99%); WEDD 7.0%, 5.7%, 9.2%, 6.3%, 7.2% - i.e. "very good" (4.6-7) to "good" (7-11.2) distortion bands, meaning the main FECG components including P and T survive. Paired t-test found no significant difference between reconstructed and original signals for any subject (P > 0.1). QRS DETECTION (Table II), reported as F1 / PPV / Sensitivity with number of test signals: train A&D -> test A&D FECG: 99.7 +/- 0.4 (97.8-99.9) / 99.6 +/- 0.7 (97.4-99.9) / 99.4 +/- 0.6 (98.8-99.7), n = 5; train A&D -> test NI-FECG: 97.9 (96.5-98.4) / 97.2 (95.9-97.9) / 96.8 (95.4-98.1), n = 14; train A&D -> test NI-FECG challenge: 94.7 (92.6-96.5) / 93.9 (92.6-95.3) / 93.9 (91.6-95.2), n = 69; train NI-FECG -> test NI-FECG (4-fold): 99.6 +/- 1.3 (98.2-99.9) / 99.6 +/- 0.6 (98.9-99.9) / 99.6 +/- 0.9 (98.8-99.9), n = 14; train Challenge -> test Challenge (4-fold): 99.3 +/- 1.3 (95.3-99.9) / 99.1 +/- 1.0 (95.3-99.9) / 99.2 +/- 0.6 (96.1-99.9), n = 69. COMPARISON (Table III), all F1: template subtraction + echo-state RNN 97.2, TS+PCA 95.4, LMS 95.4, RLS 95.9 (all Behar et al., 1 abdominal channel, 50 ms window, n = 14); OBACKC 95.6; SVD-SW 99.4 on A&D but only n = 2; Encoder-Decoder 94.1 on A&D, n = 5; Behar et al. 95.9 and Varanini et al. 99.0 on the Challenge, both using 4 abdominal channels, n = 69. The proposed...

**G. Limitations.** Author-acknowledged: model complexity is high for embedded deployment (they defer to future GPU efficiency), and a larger sample is needed to show the approach works in all circumstances; they also concede outright that "QRS detection is not the primary task supported by the proposed method" and that the comparison with Warmerdam et al. is weak because that work reported only accuracy. Reviewer-identified, in order of severity for our purposes. (1) The detector is Pan & Tompkins, not learned - so the paper offers no detector architecture, and every reported F1 is the composite of extractor quality and a classical peak-picker whose parameters are never given. (2) Only 5 subjects worldwide have real continuous fetal ground truth; for the other two datasets the training target was synthesised from the reference annotations using Daubechies wavelets, so within-dataset training partly learns to reproduce a synthetic template whose peaks sit exactly at the evaluation labels, and no safeguard confining target synthesis to training folds is described. (3) The 4-fold CV on NI-FECG and the Challenge is never stated to be record-wise or subject-wise; the 4.6-point gap between within-dataset (99.3) and cross-dataset (94.7) results on the same 69 Challenge records is direct evidence that those within-dataset numbers are optimistic. (4) Record selection: 14 of 55 NI-FECG and 69 of 75 Challenge records, plus an admission that ten retained low-amplitude noisy records degrade badly. (5)...

**H. Relevance.** Directly relevant on four axes. FIRST, the input contract is identical to ours: one abdominal channel, resampled to 200 Hz, 1-second windows of 200 samples, z-scored - we can adopt this window and rate for the Takens embedding without further justification, and cite this paper for it. SECOND, the paper's core mechanistic claim is exactly the problem our TDA hypothesis is meant to solve: plain convolution cannot separate fetal from maternal QRS because a kernel that amplifies one amplifies the other, and the fix is a masking/gating mechanism applied before the convolutions. Their attention mask is worth about +0.17 R-squared in ablation - the single largest effect in the paper - which tells us that whatever representation we choose, an explicit maternal-suppression stage is where the performance lives. Persistence images offer an alternative route to the same end (maternal and fetal cycles occupy different loops in the delay-embedded point cloud), so this paper is both our motivation and our strongest baseline. THIRD, it defines the evaluation protocol we should copy verbatim so our numbers are comparable to Table III: 30 ms matching tolerance (6 samples at 200 Hz), Sensitivity/PPV/F1 with 95% CI, LOSO on A&D FECG's 5 records, external validation on NI-FECG and CinC 2013 Set-A with the same 6 records excluded, and WEDD/ICC/Bland-Altman if we ever reconstruct a waveform. FOURTH, it sets the performance bar honestly: 99.7 F1 in-dataset on A&D under LOSO, but 97.9 on NI-FECG...

**I. Research opportunity.** Five open gaps, the first two of which are directly our project's thesis. (1) NO LEARNED DETECTOR. The paper's own admission that QRS detection is bolted on via Pan & Tompkins means nobody has asked whether an end-to-end learned fetal-QRS detection head on top of this extractor - or replacing it - would do better. Every F1 in the paper is capped by a 1985 peak-picker's behaviour on a reconstructed signal. A learned per-sample detection head, TDA-fed or not, evaluated under their exact protocol, is an untaken step. (2) NO TOPOLOGICAL OR NON-CONVOLUTIONAL REPRESENTATION. The paper diagnoses the failure mode - convolution amplifies mother and fetus together - and patches it with attention. It never asks whether a representation in which the maternal and fetal cardiac cycles are separable by construction (delay embedding, where the two quasi-periodic sources trace loops of different persistence) would remove the need for the patch. That is precisely our hypothesis, and this paper supplies both the motivation and the ablation baseline (attention on vs off) to test it against. (3) UNRESOLVED PROTOCOL AMBIGUITY. Nobody has re-run this architecture under a stated record-wise split on NI-FECG and CinC 2013. The 99.3-vs-94.7 gap on the same 69 records is a standing invitation: a paper that reports both protocols explicitly, for both this method and a new one, would be a genuine contribution on its own. (4) NO CONTROLLED HEAD-TO-HEAD. Table III compares numbers lifted from papers...

**Số liệu then chốt (kèm nguồn trong bài):**

- Sampling rate after preprocessing: 200 Hz, downsampled from 1000 Hz (Sec. 2.2 Preprocessing)
- Window length M = 200 samples = 1.0 s (Sec. 2.2, 'M refers to the number of samples, which is set to 200 for this paper')
- Band-pass cut-offs 1 Hz and 100 Hz (Sec. 2.2)
- First and last 10 s of each record truncated (Sec. 2.2)
- Generator attention branch: Conv1D filters = 1, kernel = 31 (Fig. 1, bottom-left)
- MultiHead self-attention: 2 heads, embedding dimension = window size = 200 (Fig. 1, bottom-left)
- Attention mask clipped to [0.8, 1] after z-score normalisation (Fig. 1 'Clip [0.8,1]' and Sec. 2.3 text)
- Generator main stack, 4 Conv1D layers: (filters 13, kernel 3), (7, 5), (5, 13), (1, 3), each + sine (Fig. 1, bottom-left)
- Discriminator, 4 Conv1D layers: (filters 12, kernel 3), (7, 3), (3, 3), (1, 3), each + sine, then Dense(32) + softmax (Fig. 1, bottom-right)
- Derived receptive field of generator main stack = 21 samples = 105 ms at 200 Hz, assuming stride 1 (MY CALCULATION from Fig. 1 kernel sizes; NOT reported in the paper)
- Derived generator parameter count approx. 1.6e5, dominated by the 200-dim 2-head attention block (MY ESTIMATE from Fig. 1; NOT reported)
- Cycle-consistency weight lambda = 4 (Sec. 2.3, after Eq. 4)

#### p04 — Basak P, Sakib AHMN, Chowdhury MEH, Al-Emadi N, Yalcin HC, Pedersen S, Mahmud S, Kiranyaz S, Al-Maadeed S. "A novel deep learning technique for morphology preserved fetal ECG extraction using...

**Mức truy cập:** `full_text_read` · **Tải về:** có (`p04_Basak2024-1DCycleGAN.pdf`, 2,100,166 byte) · **Độ tin cậy trích xuất:** high

**A. Problem.** Non-invasive fetal ECG recorded from the maternal abdomen is dominated by the maternal ECG plus baseline drift, motion artefacts, power-line noise, and uterine/muscle EMG; the fetal component is very low amplitude and its R-peaks frequently overlap the maternal ones. The authors argue that classical extraction methods (adaptive filters, LMS/RLS, Kalman/extended-state Kalman, ICA/PCA blind source separation, EMD/EEMD, compressive sensing) either fail under QRS coincidence and coloured noise, require a reference signal or a specific electrode montage, or - most importantly for their framing - recover only usable QRS spikes while destroying the rest of the waveform. Since clinical assessment of arrhythmia, septal defects, aortic stenosis and hypoxia needs P waves, ST segments, T waves and PR/ST/QT intervals, not just R-peak timing, the stated problem is: reconstruct the FULL, morphology-preserving fetal ECG waveform from the abdominal maternal recording, and only then derive fQRS, FHR and HRV from it.

**B. Data.** Two PhysioNet databases, combined. Dataset 1 - "Abdominal and Direct Fetal ECG Database" (ADFECGDB, Jezewski et al. 2012): 5 records from 5 women at 38-41 weeks; each record has ~5 minutes of 4-channel abdominal mECG (four electrodes around the navel, reference above the pubic symphysis, common-mode reference on the left leg, Ag-AgCl with skin abrasion) plus a SIMULTANEOUS single-channel fetal ECG recorded invasively from a fetal scalp electrode, plus expert-annotated fetal R-peak locations. Sampling 1000 Hz, 16-bit, bandwidth 1-150 Hz. Dataset 2 - Matonia et al., "Fetal electrocardiograms, direct and abdominal with reference heartbeat annotations", split by the authors into 2a (labor) and 2b (pregnancy). Dataset 2a: 4-channel abdominal mECG from 12 women (12 records, 5 minutes each) sampled at 500 Hz, with simultaneous scalp fECG sampled at 1000 Hz and expert-annotated fQRS positions. Dataset 2b: 4-channel abdominal mECG from 10 women at 32-42 weeks (10 records, 20 minutes each) at 500 Hz, with expert-labelled fetal R-peak positions ONLY - no scalp fECG, hence no regression target. Ground truth construction: for the generator, the ground truth waveform IS the invasively recorded fetal scalp signal (after the same preprocessing chain); for fQRS evaluation, the ground truth is the expert R-peak annotation shipped with the databases. Maximum distinct subjects across all three subsets is 27 women, but the paper never states the number of 1-second segments produced, the...

**C. Method.** A 1D adaptation of CycleGAN (Zhu et al. 2017) treating mECG and fECG as two signal domains. Two generators are learned, G1: mECG -> fECG and G2: fECG -> mECG, constrained to be approximate inverses by a cycle-consistency L1 loss (G1(G2(y)) ~ y and G2(G1(x)) ~ x). Two PatchGAN discriminators D1 and D2 judge realism of the generated signals. The authors' key departure from vanilla CycleGAN is the loss: they replace the log adversarial loss with a least-squares (MSE) term, then add three ECG-specific terms - a spectral loss based on the Pearson correlation of power spectral densities, a temporal loss equal to 1 - PCC between generated and ground-truth waveform, and a power loss equal to the relative absolute difference in signal power - weighted 2, 4 and 1 respectively. Training follows a standard alternating mini-batch scheme (Algorithm 1): draw minibatches from both domains, compute discriminator losses on real and generated signals, update discriminators, compute the composite generator losses for both directions, update generators. At inference only G1 is used; the reconstructed fetal waveform is then passed to a modified Engelse-Zeelenberg (EngZee) classical QRS detector to obtain fQRS positions, from which RR intervals, FHR, sigma_HR, RMSSD, SDNN/RMSSD and PNN50 are computed. Note the pipeline is fully PAIRED (scalp fECG is time-aligned with the abdominal mECG) even though CycleGAN is an unpaired-translation framework - the cycle-consistency machinery is therefore not...

**D. Model.** GENERATOR (best = "ResNet 13 blocks"). Input tensor N x 512 x 4 (batch x 1-second window x 4 abdominal channels), values in [0,1]. Encoder: padding, then three Conv1d downsampling stages with channel widths 16 -> 32 -> 64, each followed by BatchNorm and a dense layer with ReLU. Bottleneck: n = 13 residual blocks. Each residual block contains two units; each unit is ReflectionPad (written "ReflectionPad2d" in the text, a 2D leftover) -> Conv1d with 64 channels -> BatchNorm, with a fully-connected layer and a dropout layer sitting between the two units, and an identity shortcut added element-wise from the block input to the second unit's output (y = F(x,{W_i}) + x, with a linear projection W_s when shapes differ). Decoder: three ConvTranspose1d stages, shown in Figure 8(b) as 64 -> 32 -> 16 (the prose says "16, 32 and 64", an internal contradiction); BatchNorm + dense + ReLU after the first two, dense + tanh after the third. Output tensor N x 512 x 1. DISCRIMINATOR (best = "basic", a 3x3 PatchGAN): first layer Conv4; then three blocks of {dense, Conv4, normalization} (Figure 8(c) labels the normalisation as InstanceNormalization and the activations as LeakyReLU, with a "Constant" padding layer preceding each block); final Conv4 preceded by a dense layer with LeakyReLU. Alternative "Self-Discriminator" = 1x1 PatchGAN built from 1D Self-ONN (self-organised operational neural network) layers with generative neurons. Alternative generators tried: UNet-128, UNet-256, Self-FPN....

**E. Evaluation.** The paper states only: "The datasets were split using the 5-Fold cross-validation technique." It never says whether folds are formed over segments, records, or subjects. LEAKAGE RISK: HIGH. Reasoning: Table 5 reports, for each of the five folds, the GROUND-TRUTH heart-rate statistics of that fold's test set, and they are essentially identical across all five folds - mu_RR 473.18/473.18/473.19/473.18/473.20 ms, mu_HR 128.52/128.52/128.51/128.52/128.53 bpm, sigma_HR 14.14/14.14/14.15/14.14/14.14 bpm, PNN50 40.45/40.45/40.45/40.44/40.45%. If the five folds held out different RECORDS or SUBJECTS, the ground-truth fetal heart rate of each test fold would differ by several bpm at minimum, because individual fetuses differ substantially in baseline rate and variability. Values agreeing to the second decimal place are only consistent with each test fold being a random sample of 1-second segments drawn from the same pooled, multi-subject population - i.e. a random segment-level split in which every subject appears in both training and test. This is aggravated by two design choices: (i) segmentation uses OVERLAPPING windows, so near-duplicate segments can straddle the train/test boundary, and (ii) fetal ECG morphology and heart rate are strongly subject-specific, so a per-subject memorising generator would score well. The reported PCC 0.88 and F1 0.964 should therefore be read as within-subject, seen-fetus performance and are almost certainly optimistic relative to a...

**F. Results.** Architecture sweep (Table 1, PCC / spectral correlation / spectral RMSE, means over 5 folds): ResNet-13 + Basic 0.882 / 0.894 / 0.428 (best); ResNet-13 + Self 0.871 / 0.890 / 0.453; ResNet-9 + Basic 0.864 / 0.872 / 0.472; ResNet-9 + Self 0.855 / 0.869 / 0.481; UNet-256 + Basic 0.780 / 0.815 / 0.529; UNet-256 + Self 0.770 / 0.816 / 0.529; Self-FPN + Basic 0.762 / 0.805 / 0.504; UNet-128 + Basic 0.751 / 0.799 / 0.540; Self-FPN + Self 0.738 / 0.779 / 0.522; UNet-128 + Self 0.732 / 0.770 / 0.545. Fold-wise best model (Table 2): RMSE 0.106/0.109/0.103/0.102/0.115, MAE 0.069/0.073/0.068/0.069/0.077, PCC 0.89/0.88/0.89/0.89/0.87, spectral correlation 0.90/0.88/0.90/0.90/0.89, spectral RMSE 0.42/0.45/0.42/0.40/0.45. fQRS detection on the reconstructed signal with EngZee and a 31.25 ms tolerance (Table 3): per-fold precision 0.98/0.97/0.98/0.97/0.98, recall 0.95/0.95/0.95/0.95/0.94, F1 0.96/0.96/0.97/0.96/0.97, accuracy 0.93/0.92/0.93/0.92/0.92; averages precision 0.976, recall 0.948, F1 0.964, accuracy 0.926. HRV (Table 5, averages): mu_RR 474.464 ms vs ground truth 473.188 ms (0.27% error); mu_HR 128.852 vs 128.52 bpm (0.25% error); sigma_HR 18.244 vs 14.142 bpm (29% error); SDNN/RMSSD 0.696 vs 0.700 (0.57% error); PNN50 41.386% vs 40.448% (2.32% error). Reported mean fetal HR ~129 +/- 18 bpm, inside the normal 120-160 bpm band. Ablations: the composite loss raises PCC by ~10 percentage points (Figure 11a - without-loss bars sit near 0.79-0.85 per fold, with-loss bars near...

**G. Limitations.** (1) Requires FOUR abdominal channels; there is no single-channel variant or ablation on channel count, so nothing here transfers directly to a single-lead setting. (2) The 5-fold split is almost certainly segment-level random within the same subjects (see E_evaluation), so all headline numbers are within-subject; no LOSO, no held-out record, no cross-dataset or external validation. (3) Very small and homogeneous subject pool (at most 27 women, all late gestation or labor, all with an invasive scalp electrode already in place - a population by definition not representative of routine antenatal monitoring). (4) Dataset 2b has no scalp fECG and therefore cannot supply a regression target, yet the paper never clarifies whether or how it was used; segment counts and per-dataset breakdowns are absent. (5) The fQRS detector is a 1979 classical algorithm bolted on afterwards, with no comparison to alternatives and no learned detection head - so the paper does not actually answer "what is the optimal detector architecture". (6) sigma_HR is off by 29%, meaning beat-to-beat timing jitter is introduced even when waveform PCC is high; this is the very quantity HRV analysis depends on, so "morphology preserved" does not imply "timing preserved". (7) Reporting "accuracy" for peak detection is not meaningful (TN undefined). (8) The 31.25 ms tolerance differs from the conventional 50 ms used by most of the Table 4 baselines, so the comparison table is not like-for-like and the authors do...

**H. Relevance.** Useful to us as a preprocessing and backbone reference, not as a baseline. Concretely applicable: (a) The full preprocessing recipe - 512 Hz resampling, 50 Hz notch, 0.1-70 Hz high-order Butterworth applied with the apply-flip-reapply zero-phase trick (scipy.signal.filtfilt), polynomial baseline subtraction, moving-average smoothing, 1-second (512-sample) overlapping windows with edge-ripple trimming, per-segment re-baselining, and per-segment min-max normalisation to [0,1]. The per-segment normalisation matters especially for us: Takens embedding and persistent homology are scale-sensitive, and normalising each window makes persistence diagrams and persistence images comparable across windows, records and subjects. (b) The window length choice: 512 samples = 1 s at 512 Hz contains ~2 fetal beats at 129 bpm, which is a sensible unit for both a CNN receptive field and a Takens point cloud; their empirical selection of 512 Hz gives us a defensible sampling rate to standardise on. (c) The backbone evidence: a residual encoder-bottleneck-decoder with widths 16-32-64 and 9-13 residual blocks and only ~0.34 M parameters beat UNet-128/256 and Self-FPN by 0.10-0.15 PCC. That says small 1D ResNets are the right family for this signal and bounds how big our detector needs to be - a useful prior when our dataset is tiny. (d) The loss-engineering result: adding a temporal Pearson-correlation term, a PSD-correlation term and a power-matching term to the base loss bought ~10 PCC points....

**I. Research opportunity.** Several gaps this paper leaves wide open and that our project can occupy. (1) SINGLE-CHANNEL: the method needs 4 abdominal leads; there is no single-channel result at all, and the spatial redundancy across channels is doing much of the maternal-suppression work. A single-channel detector evaluated on the same records is an unaddressed and harder problem. (2) SUBJECT-INDEPENDENT EVALUATION: nobody in this paper reports leave-one-subject-out or record-wise results on ADFECGDB/Matonia; simply re-running an equivalent pipeline under a leakage-free protocol and reporting the drop would be a genuine contribution and gives us a defensible baseline number. (3) NO LEARNED DETECTOR: fQRS is found by a 1979 heuristic applied to a reconstructed waveform - the paper never asks whether a learned detection head (or our TDA + CNN head) applied directly to the raw abdominal signal beats extract-then-threshold. That is exactly the comparison our project is set up to make, and this paper supplies the extract-then-classical number (F1 0.964, though leakage-inflated) to compare against. (4) NO TDA, NO NON-EUCLIDEAN FEATURES: the paper's own failure mode - 29% error in sigma_HR, i.e. timing jitter surviving a high-PCC reconstruction - is precisely where a topological representation of the delay-embedded trajectory could help, because persistent homology of a Takens embedding encodes recurrent cycle structure rather than pointwise amplitude, and is insensitive to the amplitude-scale errors that...

**Số liệu then chốt (kèm nguồn trong bài):**

- Best model PCC = 0.882 (Table 1, ResNet-13 generator + basic discriminator, mean of 5 folds)
- Average PCC = 0.884 and spectral correlation = 0.894 (abstract and conclusion; Table 2 per-fold PCC 0.89/0.88/0.89/0.89/0.87)
- Spectral RMSE = 0.428 (Table 1, best model)
- Per-fold RMSE 0.106/0.109/0.103/0.102/0.115 and MAE 0.069/0.073/0.068/0.069/0.077 (Table 2)
- fQRS precision = 0.976, recall = 0.948, F1 = 0.964, accuracy = 0.926 (Table 3, Average row; also abstract and conclusion)
- fQRS per-fold F1 = 0.96/0.96/0.97/0.96/0.97 and accuracy = 0.93/0.92/0.93/0.92/0.92 (Table 3)
- R-peak match tolerance = 31.25 ms, i.e. 16 samples at 512 Hz (Section 4.2 text)
- mu_RR error = 0.27% (474.464 ms predicted vs 473.188 ms ground truth) and mu_HR error = 0.25% (128.852 vs 128.52 bpm) (Table 5, Average and Error rows)
- sigma_HR error = 29% (18.244 bpm predicted vs 14.142 bpm ground truth) - the model's worst HRV metric (Table 5)
- SDNN/RMSSD error = 0.57% and PNN50 error = 2.32% (Table 5, Error row)
- Ground-truth HRV per fold is essentially identical across all 5 folds: mu_RR 473.18-473.20 ms, mu_HR 128.51-128.53 bpm, sigma_HR 14.14-14.15 bpm, PNN50 40.44-40.45% (Table 5) - my evidence for segment-level (leaky) fold construction
- Generator/discriminator sweep PCC (Table 1): ResNet-13+Basic 0.882, ResNet-13+Self 0.871, ResNet-9+Basic 0.864, ResNet-9+Self 0.855, UNet-256+Basic 0.780, UNet-256+Self 0.770, Self-FPN+Basic 0.762, UNet-128+Basic 0.751, Self-FPN+Self 0.738, UNet-128+Self 0.732

#### p05 — Shokouhmand A, Tavassolian N. Fetal Electrocardiogram Extraction Using Dual-Path Source Separation of Single-Channel Non-Invasive Abdominal Recordings. IEEE Trans Biomed Eng 70(1):283-295, 2023. DOI...

**Mức truy cập:** `full_text_read` · **Tải về:** có (`p05_Shokouhmand2023-DPSS.pdf`, 5,169,096 byte) · **Độ tin cậy trích xuất:** high

**A. Problem.** Non-invasive fetal heart-rate/fetal-ECG monitoring from a SINGLE abdominal ECG channel. The abdominal recording is an under-determined mixture of maternal ECG (10-100x larger), fetal ECG, uterine/muscle activity, fetal-movement artifacts and noise; classical BSS (PCA/piCA/ICA) needs #sensors >= #sources, adaptive filters (LMS/RLS/Kalman) need a maternal reference channel or fail when maternal and fetal QRS coincide, and template subtraction assumes stationary, independent maternal cycles. The authors reframe single-channel FECG extraction as a supervised, mask-based source-separation problem so that only one electrode pair is needed - the prerequisite for a wearable at-home patch.

**B. Data.** Three PhysioNet databases. (1) FECGSYNDB (fetal ECG synthetic database): 10 simulated pregnancies, 32 abdominal channels each, 145.8 h total, 5-min records at 250 Hz, with separately provided ground-truth MECG and FECG plus non-stationary events (fetal movement, uterine contractions, HR acceleration/deceleration, ectopic beats); pregnancy-specific noise mixed in to give SNR in (-9, +2) dB. All 32 channels used. Ground truth = the simulator's own clean FECG/MECG waveforms (and their annotations); R-peaks compared using a modified Pan-Tompkins run on both reference and extracted FECG. (2) ADFECGDB: 22 real cases (10 pregnancy + 12 labor), 38-42 weeks gestation, 4 abdominal channels at 500 Hz; labor records 5 min, pregnancy records 20 min; ground truth = simultaneously recorded DIRECT fetal scalp electrode FECG; pregnancy fQRS annotations produced by an automated fetal QRS detector and validated by medical experts. (3) NIFECGC (PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2013 set A): 4-channel abdominal ECG at 1 kHz, 1-min records, manually annotated by expert clinicians; 75 records of which a33, a38, a47, a52, a54, a71, a74 were excluded for inaccurate annotations (paper reports 69 recordings used; 75 - 7 = 68, an internal inconsistency). FECGSYNDB and NIFECGC were FFT-resampled to 500 Hz. Only one channel per subject (the one with the most evident fetal components) was used, listed in supplementary Tables I-II.

**C. Method.** DPSS (dual-path source separation), a two-stage supervised pipeline. Stage 1 - denoising: a conditional least-squares GAN (LSCGAN) whose generator is Conv1D(k=3, 16 filters) -> one DP-LSTM module -> depth-wise Conv(k=3, 1 filter), trained to map noisy AECG to clean AECG; the discriminator is a stack of m 1-D residual blocks + FC. Stage 2 - mask-based separation on the denoised AECG: a GoogLeNet-style inception module with dimensionality reduction (parallel 1/3/5-tap convolutions plus a max-pool path, best configuration uses six filter lengths) produces a "sparse feature map" K x M x L; three cascaded DP-LSTM blocks then predict, via a 2-D Conv(k=3, 2M filters) + sigmoid, a K x 2M x L mask that is split into a fetal and a maternal K x M x L mask; each mask multiplies the sparse feature map elementwise and a 2-D depth-wise Conv (1 filter) collapses the M channels back to a 1-D segment, giving simultaneous FECG and MECG estimates that are reassembled by merging the overlapping 4-s segments. The DP-LSTM itself splits an M x T sequence into K sub-segments of length L and alternates an intra-segment (local) Bi-LSTM + FC + normalisation + residual with an inter-segment (global) Bi-LSTM + FC + normalisation + residual, exactly the DPRNN idea imported from speech separation, so that a 4-s signal is modelled at 100 ms and 4 s scales without long-sequence vanishing gradients.

**D. Model.** Input: 1 x 2000 (4 s @ 500 Hz), z-scored. Internal segmentation: K = 40 sub-segments of L = 50 samples (L = 100 ms, T = K*L). Denoiser generator: Conv1D k=3 with M=16 filters -> DP-LSTM (Bi-LSTM local + Bi-LSTM global, each followed by FC restoring M channels, a learned per-channel normalisation with gamma, beta in R^{M x 1}, and a residual skip) -> depth-wise Conv1D k=3, 1 filter -> denoised 1 x 2000. Discriminator: m 1-D ResBlocks, each Conv1(16) -> BN+ReLU -> Conv3(16) -> BN+ReLU -> Conv1(64) -> BN with a parallel Conv1(64)+BN shortcut, summed then ReLU; ResBlocks -> flatten -> FC -> real/fake (m not stated). Separator inception (Fig. 5a): four parallel paths in the drawn figure - Conv1(32); Conv1(32)->Conv3(32); Conv1(32)->Conv5(32); MaxPool3->Conv1(32) - concatenated + ReLU; the best ablated configuration SF6 uses six filter lengths (given as 1,3,5,7,9,11 in Sec. IV-C but 1,3,5,7,9,13 in Sec. IV-E), so M = 32 x #paths (M = 128 for the 4-path drawing; inferred from Fig. 5, not stated numerically). Mask stack: 3 cascaded DP-LSTM blocks -> 2-D Conv k=3 with 2M filters -> sigmoid -> split into two K x M x L masks -> elementwise product with the sparse feature map -> two 2-D Conv1(1 filter) depth-wise heads -> two K x 1 x L outputs -> reassembled to FECG and MECG. Bi-LSTM hidden size H, m, and total parameter count are NOT reported. Training: denoiser and separator trained SEPARATELY ("for computational simplicity"); 200 epochs; Adam, initial LR 0.001 reduced by factor 0.98...

**E. Evaluation.** FECGSYNDB: leave-one-SUBJECT-out cross-validation, 10 folds - train on the augmented data of 9 simulated pregnancies (3,432,960 segments), test on the held-out pregnancy (190,720 segments). This is subject-independent, so LEAKAGE RISK IS LOW for the split itself; the paper even acknowledges that the synthetic result is optimistic because "the test set is sampled from the same dataset and thus the same data distribution as the training set". Two caveats: (i) the 90%-overlapping windows make the 190,720 test segments massively correlated, so the reported standard deviations are over folds (n=10), not over independent segments - segment counts must not be read as independent samples; (ii) all 32 channels of a subject stay within the same fold, which is correct. Real data: the model trained on FECGSYNDB is applied to ADFECGDB(P) (2,999 segments/channel), ADFECGDB(L) (749) and NIFECGC (149) with NO fine-tuning and NO real-data training - i.e. synthetic-to-real, cross-dataset transfer, the LOWEST-leakage setting in this literature; ADFECGDB additionally has an independent gold standard (fetal scalp electrode). One optimistic-bias source remains: the single abdominal channel per subject is chosen as "the channel with the most evident fetal ECG components" (supplementary Tables I-II) - a selection made with knowledge of the signal, which favours the method on real data and would not be available blind in deployment (the paper suggests an SQI could automate it, but does not...

**F. Results.** FECGSYNDB LOSO: F1 99.03 +/- 0.39%, Sen 98.55 +/- 0.59%, PPV 99.52 +/- 0.29%, Acc 98.08 +/- 0.77%, SI-SNR +14.92 +/- 1.02 dB (Table I). Zero-shot on real data (Table II): ADFECGDB(P) F1 97.24% / FHR precision 87.88%; ADFECGDB(L) F1 98.08% / 89.22%; ADFECGDB combined F1 97.7%, Sen 97.3%, PPV 98.09%, Acc 95.53%, FHR precision 88.61%; NIFECGC F1 95.3%, Sen 94.2%, PPV 96.5%, Acc 91.34%, FHR precision 83.86%. Ablation (Table III, F1 on FECGSYNDB): 1 DP baseline 61.76 +/- 1.52; +denoiser 84.21 +/- 1.06 (+22.45 points); 2 DP + DN 84.10; 3 DP + DN 85.13 +/- 0.96 (best DP count); 4 DP + DN 82.1 (overfits); adding sparse features - SF3 88.73, SF4 90.16, SF5 93.07, SF6 94.68 +/- 0.39 (best), SF7 92.43, SF8 91.77 (overfits); + data augmentation 99.03 +/- 0.39 (+4.35 points). Window sweeps (Fig. 9): segment length swept 1-10 s, best 4 s with F1 98.37%, falling to 90.39% at 10 s; sub-segment length best at 100 ms (99.03%) vs 97.11% (50 ms), 98.03% (150 ms), 97.91% (200 ms), 94.65% (250 ms). Separability (Fig. 8): purity 0.9992/0.9846/0.9412, Jaccard 0.9984/0.9779/0.9352, Davies-Bouldin 0.7156/0.7318/0.7813 on FECGSYNDB/ADFECGDB/NIFECGC. Competitors on NIFECGC in Table IV: RCED-Net 93.62, STFT+GAN 93.02, CycleGAN 94.7, STFT+NMF 83.8, clustering (Zhang) 98.07, TF+Shannon 98.67, Behar 4-ch 96, Varanini 4-ch 98.99. Runtime for 4 s of signal: 0.52 s Intel 3.6 GHz, 1.2 s Broadcom BCM2711, 1.8 s Snapdragon S1, 1.0 s Apple S7 (all < 4 s, so real-time capable).

**G. Limitations.** (1) No learned fetal-QRS detector at all - detection is a modified Pan-Tompkins applied post hoc to the extracted FECG, and the peak-picking stage is never ablated or tuned, so an unknown share of the residual error is detector error rather than separation error. (2) Trained exclusively on synthetic FECGSYNDB; the paper concedes the synthetic number is inflated by same-distribution testing, and on real data sensitivity is the weak metric (94.2% NIFECGC) with FHR precision only 83.86%. (3) The abdominal channel is manually selected per subject; the proposed SQI-based automation is described but not implemented or evaluated. (4) Documented failure modes: the GAN denoiser can amplify artifacts into fetal-QRS-like peaks (false positives) and can smooth away true fetal QRS under high-energy noise (false negatives); the extracted FECG shows oscillatory inter-beat artifacts that contaminate fetal P and T waves, worsened by residual maternal QRS. (5) Sedentary subjects only - ambulatory/motion noise is explicitly deferred to future work (planned DiscoGAN-based domain adaptation), as is the case where maternal and fetal RR intervals coincide over long periods. (6) Reporting gaps: no parameter count, no FLOPs, no Bi-LSTM hidden size H, no number of discriminator ResBlocks m, no confidence intervals or significance tests, no per-record results in the main text. (7) Internal inconsistencies: inception filter sizes {1,3,5,7,9,11} (Sec. IV-C) vs {1,3,5,7,9,13} (Sec. IV-E); 75 - 7 = 68...

**H. Relevance.** Very high, in three ways. (a) Baseline: it is the single-channel state of the art our TDA pipeline must be compared against - F1 97.7% (ADFECGDB, 22 records) and 95.3% (NIFECGC, 69 records) from one channel, plus a ready-made Table IV of competitor numbers under the same +/-50 ms rule. (b) Transferable constants: 500 Hz, 4-s / 2000-sample windows with 90% overlap, 2 Hz zero-phase Butterworth HPF + 2nd-order 50/60 Hz notch (Q=25), per-window z-scoring, +/-50 ms TP tolerance, FHR precision at +/-5 BPM, LOSO on synthetic then zero-shot to ADFECGDB and NIFECGC - copying these makes our numbers directly comparable and defends us against leakage criticism. (c) Conceptual: the empirically optimal two-scale decomposition (100 ms local unit inside a 4 s global context) is precisely the scale pair our Takens-embedding + persistent-homology features should target - a 100 ms sub-window is roughly one fetal QRS, and 4 s is ~8-10 fetal beats. Their quantified ablation also gives us a prior on where the gains live: denoising is worth ~22 F1 points, multi-scale (inception) feature extraction ~10 points, augmentation ~4 points, depth beyond 3 recurrent blocks hurts. Finally, their explicit failure analysis (denoiser-created pseudo-peaks, smoothed-out real peaks) is exactly the kind of error a topological gate on the raw signal could catch, because persistence features of the raw embedding do not depend on a generative denoiser.

**I. Research opportunity.** The paper optimises EXTRACTION and then hands the extracted waveform to a 1985-vintage peak picker; it never asks whether the detector is the bottleneck. Concretely open: (1) no learned or topological fQRS detector head, and no ablation of peak-picking at all - a direct opening for "same extraction front-end, better detector" and for a detector that consumes the raw single channel without waveform reconstruction; (2) the reported false positives come from a generative denoiser inventing fetal-looking peaks - a detector built on persistence images of the Takens embedding is not driven by a generator and could act as a verification/gating stage, and combining DPSS-style masks with a TDA confidence score is untried; (3) sensitivity on real data (94.2% NIFECGC) and FHR precision (83.86%) are far below the synthetic ceiling, so the synthetic-to-real gap is the real target, and no domain adaptation to real AECG was done (only proposed); (4) automatic channel/lead selection via an SQI is proposed but never implemented or evaluated - a topological signal-quality index is a natural, publishable contribution; (5) no per-record or per-SNR breakdown, no uncertainty estimate, and no analysis of the coincident maternal/fetal QRS case (which they name as their main unresolved failure) - persistent homology of the delay embedding is a plausible discriminator exactly when two periodicities overlap in time; (6) no parameter/FLOP budget - a small TDA+CNN detector could claim a compute win...

**Số liệu then chốt (kèm nguồn trong bài):**

- F1 99.03% +/- 0.39 on FECGSYNDB (leave-one-subject-out, 10 folds) - Abstract, Table I 'Average' row, and Sec. IV-C
- Sen 98.55 +/- 0.59%, PPV 99.52 +/- 0.29%, Acc 98.08 +/- 0.77%, SI-SNR +14.92 +/- 1.02 dB on FECGSYNDB - Table I 'Average' row
- Best FECGSYNDB fold = subject 8: Sen 99.09, PPV 99.61, Acc 98.71, F1 99.35%, SI-SNR +15.94 dB - Table I and Sec. IV-C text
- Worst FECGSYNDB fold = subject 2: F1 98.24%, SI-SNR +13.35 dB - Table I and Sec. IV-C text
- ADFECGDB (22 records, combined labor+pregnancy): F1 97.7%, Sen 97.3%, PPV 98.09%, Acc 95.53%, FHR precision 88.61% - Table II and Table IV
- ADFECGDB(P) (10 records): F1 97.24%, Sen 96.5%, PPV 97.98%, Acc 94.67%, FHR precision 87.88% - Table II
- ADFECGDB(L) (12 records): F1 98.08%, Sen 98.0%, PPV 98.18%, Acc 96.25%, FHR precision 89.22% - Table II
- NIFECGC (69 records): F1 95.3%, Sen 94.2%, PPV 96.5%, Acc 91.34%, FHR precision 83.86% - Abstract, Table II, Table IV
- Average over the two real datasets: FHR precision 86.23, Sen 95.75, PPV 97.29, Acc 93.43, F1 96.5 - Table II last row (the Sec. IV-C prose quotes these same five numbers shifted by one column, calling 86.23 'sensitivity' and 93.43 'F1')
- Ablation: baseline 1 DP-LSTM F1 61.76 +/- 1.52% -> with denoiser 84.21 +/- 1.06% (+22.45 points) - Table III rows 1-2 and Sec. IV-E
- Ablation DP count: 1 DP+DN 84.21, 2 DP+DN 84.10, 3 DP+DN 85.13 +/- 0.96 (best), 4 DP+DN 82.1 +/- 0.82 - Table III rows 2-5
- Ablation sparse-feature width: SF3 88.73, SF4 90.16, SF5 93.07, SF6 94.68 +/- 0.39 (best), SF7 92.43, SF8 91.77 - Table III rows 6-11

#### p06 — Chen L, Wu S, Zhou Z. Fetal ECG Signal Extraction from Maternal Abdominal ECG Signals Using Attention R2W-Net. DOI 10.3390/s25030601 (PMC11820586)

**Mức truy cập:** `full_text_read` · **Tải về:** có (`p06_Chen2025-R2WNet.pdf`, 5,339,935 byte) · **Độ tin cậy trích xuất:** high

**A. Problem.** Fetal ECG recorded non-invasively at the maternal abdomen (maECG) is low-amplitude, low-SNR, and is dominated by the maternal ECG (MECG), whose QRS morphology and duration resemble the fetal one; the two frequently overlap in time. Classical approaches (wavelet transform, adaptive noise cancellation, extended Kalman filter, EMD, PCA/ICA blind source separation) are parameter-sensitive, noise-fragile, often need a second (thoracic) reference channel, and ICA in particular degrades badly when the number of input channels is small. Existing deep-learning extractors still fail when MECG and FECG overlap strongly or noise is high, and frequently destroy the P and T waves that clinicians need. The paper's goal is an end-to-end model that extracts a clean, morphology-preserving FECG from a SINGLE abdominal channel, robustly enough that fetal QRS complexes can be detected reliably afterwards.

**B. Data.** Four PhysioNet databases. (1) FECGSYNDB (training + synthetic test): FECGSYN simulator, 32 abdominal + 2 chest channels, 10 pregnant subjects, 7 physiological scenarios (Baseline, C0 noise-only, C1 fetal movement, C2 MHR/FHR accel-decel, C3 uterine contractions, C4 maternal+fetal ectopic beats, C5 twins), 5 additive-noise levels (0, 3, 6, 9, 12 dB), 5 repeats each -> 1750 signals, 5 min each, sampling rate 250 Hz. Only abdominal channels 11, 19, 22, 25 used for training. Subjects 1-9 = train, subject 10 = test. Ground truth = simulator's noise-free FECG. (2) ADFECGDB (fine-tuning + main real test): 5 women, 38-41 weeks gestation, 4 abdominal channels + 1 DIRECT FETAL SCALP channel, 5 min per recording, 1 kHz. Ground truth = the invasive scalp-electrode FECG (for waveform MSE/MAE/SNR) and its R-peaks (for detection). Records reported: R01, R04, R07, R08, R10. (3) PCDB = PhysioNet/CinC Challenge 2013 set A: 75 records, 4 abdominal channels each, 60 s per record, 1 kHz. Ground truth = fetal R-peaks manually annotated by ECG experts. (4) NIFECGDB: 55 records with 3-4 abdominal + 2 thoracic channels, 1 kHz, public fetal beat annotations; only 14 records used (154, 192, 244, 274, 290, 323, 368, 444, 597, 733, 746, 811, 826, 906), chosen to match the comparison set of Ghonchi & Abolghasemi (ref 15). Number of subjects actually contributing test data: 1 synthetic subject + 5 ADFECGDB women + 75 PCDB records + 14 NIFECGDB records. All signals resampled to 250 Hz before use. No IRB...

**C. Method.** A W-shaped parallel network ("Attention R2W-Net") built from two one-dimensional Attention R2U-Nets. The right-hand U-Net is dedicated to reconstructing the MECG, the left-hand one to reconstructing the FECG. Unlike the classical sequential W-Net (two U-Nets in series, ref 18), the two branches run in PARALLEL and interact inside the encoder: at each encoder scale the MECG features produced by the right encoder are subtracted from the left (fetal) encoder path, with a tanh activation applied to the subtraction to stabilise/enhance the separation. Inside every encoder and decoder stage the plain feed-forward convolution of U-Net is replaced by a Recurrent Residual Convolution (RRC) block, and every skip connection is replaced by an additive Attention Gate that uses the deeper (decoder) feature as a gating signal to re-weight the shallower (encoder) feature before concatenation. The pipeline is: band-pass filter -> resample to 250 Hz -> segment into 1024-sample windows -> Z-score -> network -> two reconstructed waveforms -> Pan-Tompkins peak detection on the reconstructed FECG. Training is synthetic-first (FECGSYNDB) then fine-tuned on real ADFECGDB data.

**D. Model.** INPUT/OUTPUT SHAPES. Input: 1024 x 1 (single-channel maECG, 250 Hz, 4.096 s). Outputs: two 1024 x 1 waveforms (FECG from the left decoder, MECG from the right decoder). ENCODER (each of the two branches, identical topology): 5 RRC blocks interleaved with 4 max-pooling layers of size 2x1. Channel sizes stated verbatim as "64, 128, 256, 512, 256, and 1024" - six values for five levels, internally inconsistent and almost certainly a typo for 64/128/256/512/1024. Bottleneck temporal length is therefore 1024/16 = 64 samples (each bottleneck sample spans 256 ms). DECODER: symmetric - 4 upsampling layers (transposed convolution 2x1 + ReLU, per the Figure 3 legend) and 5 RRC blocks, with Attention Gates at every skip, followed by a final 1x1 convolution + ReLU to produce the single output channel. RRC BLOCK: y_ijk^l(t) = (w_k^f)^T x_l^{f(i,j)}(t) + (w_k^r)^T x_l^{r(i,j)}(t-1) + b_k, passed through ReLU, then a residual identity add x_{l+1} = x_l + F(x_l, w_l). T = 2, i.e. one general convolution layer plus two recurrent time steps per convolutional unit (Figure 4a shows two stacked Conv+ReLU units each with a recurrent self-loop, wrapped in one residual add). KERNEL SIZES: 5 in the MECG-extraction branch, 3 in the FECG-extraction branch (explicitly motivated: larger kernels for maternal context, smaller kernels for fine fetal local detail). Stride = 1 everywhere; zero-padding in all convolutions and deconvolutions to keep amplitude/length stable. No dilations used. ATTENTION GATE:...

**E. Evaluation.** SPLITS. (a) FECGSYNDB: subjects 1-9 train, subject 10 test -> genuinely SUBJECT-INDEPENDENT on the synthetic data. LOW leakage risk for that arm. (b) ADFECGDB: "the model was fine-tuned using a subset of the ADFECGDB, while the remaining data were used for model testing." Which records or which fraction is never stated. Critically, ADFECGDB contains exactly five recordings and Table 5 reports test results for ALL FIVE (R01, R04, R07, R08, R10). Therefore the "subset" used for fine-tuning cannot have been record-disjoint from the test set unless the authors trained on nothing - the only consistent reading is a within-record segment split (e.g. first part of each 5-min record fine-tunes, rest tests). That is a random/temporal segment split within the same subjects, which for a periodic physiological signal with stable per-subject fetal morphology is HIGH LEAKAGE RISK, and it is the arm that produces the headline 99.17% F1. (c) PCDB and NIFECGDB: no fine-tuning on these is described, so they read as cross-dataset external tests (LOW leakage risk) - but the paper never states this explicitly, so it is technically UNCLEAR whether any of those records were touched during training. Their lower scores (98.03 and 97.08) are consistent with true external evaluation. NO cross-validation, no leave-one-subject-out, no repeated runs, no seeds, no confidence intervals on the detection metrics (only mean +/- SD for waveform MSE/MAE on ADFECGDB and FECGSYN). NIFECGDB uses a hand-picked...

**F. Results.** FQRS DETECTION (Pan-Tompkins on extracted FECG): - ADFECGDB (Table 5, per record): R01 Se 99.46 / PPV 99.34 / F1 99.40; R04 99.14 / 99.22 / 99.18; R07 99.30 / 99.18 / 99.24; R08 99.23 / 98.94 / 99.08; R10 99.07 / 98.85 / 98.96; Total 99.24 / 99.11 / 99.17. - Table 6: FECGSYNDB Se 97.43 / PPV 97.98 / F1 97.56; ADFECGDB 99.24 / 99.11 / 99.17; PCDB 98.23 / 97.74 / 98.03; NIFECGDB 96.76 / 97.39 / 97.08. - Reported comparators (Table 6, unreliable - see limitations): RCED-Net ADFECGDB F1 94.10, PCDB 93.62; CycleGAN FECGSYNDB 96.10, ADFECGDB 99.70, PCDB 97.90; 1D-CycleGAN ADFECGDB 96.26; AEDL PCDB 98.02, NIFECGDB 92.87; DPSS ADFECGDB 97.70, PCDB 95.30; W-Net ADFECGDB 98.81, PCDB 96.91; W-NETR ADFECGDB 99.88, PCDB 98.21. EXTRACTION QUALITY: - FECGSYN by scenario (Table 1) MSE: C0 0.079, C1 0.083, C2 0.081, C3 0.122, C4 0.114, C5 0.097, total 0.096 +/- 0.016; MAE: C0 0.075, C1 0.077, C2 0.082, C3 0.093, C4 0.085, C5 0.079, total 0.082 +/- 0.006. (Worst cases = C3 uterine contractions and C4 ectopic beats.) - ADFECGDB (Table 2) per record MSE/MAE/SNR: R01 0.019/0.009/9.08; R04 0.024/0.013/7.17; R07 0.031/0.017/7.94; R08 0.025/0.010/8.41; R10 0.027/0.014/7.53; mean 0.025+/-0.004 / 0.013+/-0.003 / 8.03+/-0.67 dB. - Table 3 comparison: proposed FECGSYNDB MSE 0.096+/-0.016 / MAE 0.082+/-0.006 vs RCED-Net 0.171/0.128, PA2NET 0.249/0.172, CycleGAN 0.109/0.092; ADFECGDB 0.025+/-0.004 / 0.013+/-0.003 vs RCED-Net 0.061/0.019, PA2NET 0.146/0.098, CycleGAN 0.042/0.011, LinkNet++ 0.089/0.068,...

**G. Limitations.** 1. The R-peak detector is NOT learned - Pan-Tompkins does all the localisation, so the paper offers zero evidence about optimal detector design and the reported F1 conflates extraction quality with a hand-tuned classical detector's behaviour. 2. ADFECGDB evaluation is almost certainly not record-disjoint (fine-tune "subset" + test on all five records) -> the headline 99.17% is an in-subject number. The split is never specified, so the result is not reproducible. 3. No parameter count, FLOPs, memory footprint, receptive field, or training wall-clock time. Only a single inference-latency number, on unspecified hardware conditions. 4. Internal inconsistencies that an implementer will trip over: channel list "64, 128, 256, 512, 256, and 1024" (six values, five levels); ReLU (equations, Figures 3-4) vs Leaky ReLU (training section); F1 defined as TP/(TP+FP+FN) but reported as the harmonic mean of Se and PPV; text claims "all p-values were less than 0.05" while Table 4 contains p = 5.743e-2. 5. Table 6 comparator numbers are impossible in two rows (F1 > both Se and PPV for CycleGAN and W-NETR on ADFECGDB), and comparisons are copied from the original papers rather than re-run, so the "state-of-the-art beaten" claim is weakly supported. Citation numbers in the Discussion are also wrong ([22] and [26] used where [28] and [30] are meant). 6. NIFECGDB uses only 14 of 55 records, hand-picked to match a prior paper; PCDB uses only set A. No worst-case or per-record breakdown for...

**H. Relevance.** Directly applicable to our single-channel fECG + TDA project in five ways. (1) PROBLEM MATCH: the input is exactly our input - one abdominal channel, 1024 samples at 250 Hz. It proves that single-channel separation is feasible and gives us the current performance bar on the three databases we will use (ADFECGDB 99.17, PCDB 98.03, NIFECGDB 97.08 F1). (2) PREPROCESSING RECIPE we can copy verbatim and defend by citation: 3-100 Hz band-pass, resample to 250 Hz, 4.096 s windows with a small (96 ms) overlap, per-window Z-score. That same 250 Hz / 1024-sample window is a sensible unit for a Takens embedding too - at 250 Hz a fetal RR of ~400-500 ms is 100-125 samples, so a 4 s window holds ~8-10 fetal beats, enough for a persistence diagram to be stable while still being a real-time-sized chunk. (3) ARCHITECTURE PRIORS validated by ablation: 5 encoder levels is the sweet spot (4 underfits, 6-7 overfit); recurrent+residual convolution beats plain conv by 2.8 F1 points; attention gates beat raw skips; L1 beats L2 on noisy abdominal data by 1.25 F1 points. These are cheap, transferable defaults for whatever backbone we put after the persistence-image front end. (4) THE AUXILIARY-MECG TRICK is the single most reusable idea: predict the maternal component as a second output and subtract it (with tanh) inside the encoder. Our TDA pipeline could exploit the same asymmetry - maternal complexes are the dominant, most persistent topological loops in the Takens embedding, so an explicit...

**I. Research opportunity.** The paper stops exactly where our project starts. Four concrete gaps: (1) NO LEARNED DETECTOR. Peak picking is delegated to Pan-Tompkins, an amplitude/derivative heuristic designed for adult chest ECG. Every failure mode after extraction is therefore un-modelled. A learned detector head - and specifically one fed by topological features that are invariant to the amplitude scaling and morphology distortion that break Pan-Tompkins on residual-noise fECG - is an obvious, unclaimed improvement. Ablating "extraction + Pan-Tompkins" vs "extraction + learned head" vs "TDA head on raw abdominal signal" is a clean contribution. (2) NO AMPLITUDE-INVARIANT REPRESENTATION. The whole method is a waveform regressor trained with L1 on amplitude, which is why the ADFECGDB reference (a scalp electrode with completely different gain) is a poor target and why the authors need per-window Z-scoring. Persistent homology of a Takens embedding is naturally invariant to the things that hurt them (baseline drift, gain, lead placement), so a TDA front end attacks a weakness they explicitly report rather than one we invented. (3) NO HONEST SUBJECT-INDEPENDENT EVALUATION ON REAL DATA. Their real-data headline comes from a split that cannot be record-disjoint. A rigorous leave-one-record-out / cross-database protocol on ADFECGDB + PCDB + NIFECGDB, reported alongside a re-run of their pipeline under the same protocol, would be a genuine methodological contribution on its own - and would let us claim a...

**Số liệu then chốt (kèm nguồn trong bài):**

- Sampling rate after resampling: 250 Hz (Section 3.3.1 Preprocessing)
- Input window: 1024 samples with 24-sample overlap at both ends = 4.096 s window, 96 ms overlap (Section 3.3.1; seconds derived by me from 1024/250 and 24/250)
- Band-pass: 3 Hz high-pass, 100 Hz low-pass (Section 3.3.1)
- Normalisation: Z-score (Section 3.3.1)
- Encoder: 5 RRC blocks + 4 max-pooling layers; decoder: 4 upsampling layers + 5 RRC blocks (Section 3.3.2 Encoder and Decoder)
- Channel sizes stated as 64, 128, 256, 512, 256, 1024 (Section 3.3.2, page 7-8 of PDF) - six values for five levels, internally inconsistent
- Kernel sizes: 5 for the MECG branch, 3 for the FECG branch (Section 3.3.2, page 7)
- RRC recurrence T = 2, i.e. one standard conv + two recurrent steps (Section 3.3.2, page 7)
- Max-pooling 2x1, transposed conv 2x1, final conv 1x1 (Figure 3 legend, page 6 of PDF)
- Stride 1 with zero-padding in all conv/deconv (Section 3.3.3 Training Parameters)
- Loss = MAE (L1); Adam; initial LR 0.0001; batch size 32; 30 epochs (Section 3.3.3)
- Activation stated as Leaky ReLU in Section 3.3.3, but ReLU in equations 2 and 4 and Figures 3-5 (internal contradiction)

#### p07 — Asadi A, Ghaffari A, Hatami Z. U-Net-Based Deep Learning Framework for Single-Channel Fetal ECG Extraction Using Time-Delay Representation (SCTD-Net / Ursa-Net). DOI 10.1109/ACCESS.2025.3639272

**Mức truy cập:** `full_text_read` · **Tải về:** không · **Độ tin cậy trích xuất:** high

**A. Problem.** Extracting the fetal ECG from a SINGLE abdominal ECG channel, where the maternal ECG dominates and where power-line interference, baseline drift, white noise, electrode-contact noise and muscle contraction overlap the FECG in both time and frequency, and the FECG has markedly lower SNR than the MECG. Single-channel is the operating point that matters for long-term / home monitoring because multi-channel electrode arrays are inconvenient for the mother, but it rules out the source-separation family (ICA, PCA, piCA, CCA/adaptive LPCA) that needs several channels, and it rules out adaptive noise cancellation, which needs a separate thoracic reference lead. Template subtraction does work single-channel but requires prior, accurate maternal-QRS localisation and fails when fetal and maternal QRS amplitudes are similar or the complexes overlap in time. Prior deep-learning work (RCED-Net, AECG-DecompNet, W-Net, DAW-Net, Attention R2W-Net) feeds the raw 1D single-channel signal to 1D CNNs; the authors argue that collapsing 2D convolutions to 1D loses representational power, and that this hurts most precisely in the low-SNR fetal-extraction step. Their thesis: lift the single-channel signal into 2D with a time-delay (Takens) embedding first, then use a genuine 2D CNN.

**B. Data.** TRAINING DATA - entirely synthetic. Generated with the FECGSYN toolbox (Behar et al.), a Gaussian-ECG-model simulator of non-invasive fetal ECG that reproduces the FECGSYNDB scenarios. Each realistic abdominal-mixture simulation has 34 channels: 2 MECG reference channels plus 32 AECG channels. Because the simulator emits separate FECG, MECG and noise signals per channel, exact waveform targets are available for supervised regression - this is how ground truth was created for training (no annotation needed). Three physiological events were simulated: case 0 (baseline with noise), case 1 (fetal movement with noise), case 2 (MHR/FHR accelerations and decelerations with noise). Noise sources: muscle artefact (MA), baseline wander (BW) and electrode motion (EM). Segments were scored by SINR (Eq. 6, ratio of target energy to AECG-minus-target energy, in dB) and stratified into three quality tiers: HIGH quality 20,000 segments with SINR_f in [-5, 0] dB and SINR_m in [-5, 5] dB; MEDIUM quality 25,000 segments with SINR_f in [-15, -5] and SINR_m in [0, 10]; LOW quality 10,000 segments with SINR_f in [-25, -15] and SINR_m in [10, 15]. Total 55,000 training segments. Number of distinct simulated subjects/pregnancies, and the remaining generation parameters, are in Table 1, which is a bitmap in the OA HTML and could not be read - so those are not reported here. TEST DATA - two real, public datasets, neither of which contributed any training sample. (1) PCDB = Set A of the...

**C. Method.** A sequential two-stage framework, SCTD-Net (Single-Channel Time Delay Network), plus a single-stage ablation. SCTD-2Net (the main model): (i) preprocess the single-channel AECG (250 Hz, 3 Hz HP, 100 Hz LP, 1024-sample windows, min-max normalise then subtract the mean); (ii) a 1D U-Net regresses the MECG from the AECG; (iii) apply Takens time-delay embedding SEPARATELY to the AECG and to the estimated MECG, then INTERLEAVE their delay rows in consecutive rows to form one multidimensional matrix X - the paper is explicit that this row arrangement, which places AECG and MECG delay vectors sharing the same lag next to each other, is what lets the network learn to subtract MECG delay vectors from AECG delay vectors, and that this is where the performance improvement comes from; (iv) Ursa-Net, a custom 2D U-Net with an appended reconstruction block, consumes X and emits the 1D FECG; (v) Pan-Tompkins is run on the reconstructed FECG to obtain fetal R-peaks. SCTD-1Net (ablation): skip step (ii) entirely, embed the AECG alone, and feed that straight to Ursa-Net. The theoretical justification for the embedding is Takens' Theorem, cited as reference [60] (Takens, Dynamical Systems and Turbulence, Warwick 1980), on the grounds that the state space of a dynamical system can be reconstructed from a single observed time series. Note carefully for our purposes: the paper invokes Takens only to justify forming a lag matrix. There is NO persistent homology, NO Vietoris-Rips or sublevel-set...

**D. Model.** ARCHITECTURE 1 - 1D U-Net for MECG extraction. Input 1024 samples of AECG (~4.096 s @ 250 Hz), output 1024 samples of MECG. No fully-connected layers anywhere (the paper notes this keeps complexity down). Contracting path: 4 downsampling levels, downsampling by 1D AveragePooling with pool size 2. Channel widths across the five levels: 32, 64, 128, 256, 512. Three convolution layers at every level, kernel size 7. Each convolution is followed by batch normalisation and LeakyReLU with alpha = 0.01; each activation is followed by dropout with rate 0.1. Expansive path: symmetric, 4 upsampling levels implemented with deconvolution layers followed by convolution layers, with channel counts mirroring the contracting path. Skip connections between every corresponding level, motivated both as feature reuse and as vanishing-gradient mitigation. Head: one extra 1D convolution with kernel size 1 at the end of expansive level 1 produces the MECG. ARCHITECTURE 2 - time-delay embedding. For SCTD-2Net: AECG x_A = (x_A1 ... x_AN) with N = 1019; delay vectors of length L = 4 with unit delay step, x_Ai = (x_Ai ... x_A(i+L-1))^T for 1 <= i <= K where K = N - L + 1 = 1016; the delay matrix X_A is in R^(L x K); the MECG delay matrix X_M is built identically; the rows of X_A and X_M are then interleaved (row order x_A1, x_M1, x_A2, x_M2, ... x_AL, x_ML) to give X in R^(2L x K) with 2L = 8 and K = 1016, described in the paper as "dimensions of 1016 x 8". For SCTD-1Net: input is the AECG delay...

**E. Evaluation.** SPLIT: a strict synthetic-to-real cross-domain protocol. 100% of training data (all 55,000 segments) comes from the FECGSYN simulator; 100% of evaluation data comes from two real datasets that contribute ZERO training samples. There is no train/test split within either real dataset, and consequently no random-segment split, no subject overlap and no record overlap between training and test. Evaluation is therefore fully subject-independent and in fact fully dataset-independent. No cross-validation is performed, and none is needed under this design. Testing covers 80 single-channel AECG signals from PCDB (chosen to be identical to the sets used by RCED-Net, GAN, AECG-DecompNet, W-Net and SCTD-ICA) and 17 of the 20 abdominal channels from ADFECGDB's 5 subjects. Metrics are computed over FQRS matches within a 50 ms tolerance; Table 2 reports mean and standard deviation across recordings, though the std values themselves live inside the unreadable table image. LEAKAGE RISK: LOW - the lowest-risk category in our rubric. Reasoning: (a) train and test are disjoint by construction because the training corpus is entirely simulated and no real recording, subject or record ever enters training; (b) there is no possibility of the same subject or the same recording appearing on both sides, which is the dominant leakage mode in this literature; (c) it is also cross-dataset in the sense that two independent real corpora, acquired with different protocols and different ground-truth...

**F. Results.** HEADLINE (SCTD-2Net, the two-stage model). PCDB, 80 recordings: Se 97.19%, PPV 98.80%, F1 97.97%. ADFECGDB, 17 channels from 5 subjects: Se 95.17%, PPV 97.94%, F1 96.52%. ABLATION (two-stage vs one-stage). SCTD-2Net beats SCTD-1Net on all three metrics on both datasets, with the gain concentrated in sensitivity: approximately +1.1% Se on PCDB and +1.56% Se on ADFECGDB, which the authors read as fewer missed FQRS. Qualitatively, Figure 7 shows SCTD-1Net failing outright on a high-noise recording (a22-AECG2) and on a low-SINR recording (a01-AECG4) where SCTD-2Net still recovers the FQRS. COMPLEXITY. SCTD-1Net 7.68 M parameters / 92.78 MB / 3.91 GFLOPs; SCTD-2Net 9.22 M parameters / 111.79 MB / 4.63 GFLOPs - the authors call the two-stage overhead "modest" relative to the accuracy gain. COMPARISONS (18 methods: 7 traditional + 11 deep learning). On PCDB, SCTD-2Net exceeds AECG-DecompNet by +3.88% Se, +1.58% PPV and +2.74% F1; exceeds the 3-4 channel FastICA-AEFLN-NWT by +1.77% F1 while using a single channel and evaluating on 80 recordings instead of 16; and beats every deep-learning competitor on every metric EXCEPT Improved CycleGAN and Attention R2W-Net, against which its Se and F1 are slightly lower but its PPV is +0.56% and +1.06% higher respectively. It also improves on the authors' own earlier SCTD-ICA on both datasets. On ADFECGDB, SCTD-2Net beats all traditional methods and beats RCED-Net, GAN and CDC-Net on all metrics; Adaptive LPCA comes close but needs 4 channels;...

**G. Limitations.** AUTHORS' OWN STATED LIMITATIONS: (1) error propagation - because SCTD-2Net depends on a first MECG-extraction stage, inaccuracies in MECG reconstruction propagate into FECG separation; they propose a unified W-shaped Ursa-Net variant that jointly separates both sources as the fix. (2) Training exclusively on synthetic data improved robustness and cross-dataset generalisation but "may limit the accurate reconstruction of fine morphological details in real FECG signals"; they suggest adding real direct scalp FECG to training while reserving independent evaluation sets. (3) The model has not been evaluated under pathological conditions such as fetal arrhythmia, which they say is required before clinical translation. LIMITATIONS I IDENTIFY: (4) It is an extraction paper wearing a detection paper's clothes - all reported performance is FQRS Se/PPV/F1, but the detector is off-the-shelf Pan-Tompkins applied post hoc; the network is never trained on a detection objective, so the reported ceiling conflates reconstruction quality with the peak-picker's behaviour, and no ablation isolates the two. (5) Ablation poverty: the ONLY ablation is one-stage vs two-stage. The embedding dimension L, the delay step tau, the interleaving order, the (3,7) kernel, the asymmetric pooling schedule, the channel widths, the reconstruction-block depth, the pooling type and the loss are all fixed without justification or sensitivity analysis - so the paper offers no evidence about which of its choices...

**H. Relevance.** This is the highest-relevance paper in the set for our project, because it is the published work that comes closest to our pipeline without taking the topological step. Concretely: it takes a SINGLE abdominal channel, applies a TAKENS DELAY EMBEDDING (citing Takens 1980 explicitly), obtains a 2D representation, and feeds that to a CNN - which is our steps 1, 2 and 4. The only thing it omits is our step 3, persistent homology and persistence images. That makes it simultaneously our strongest precedent and our indispensable control condition. Directly transferable to us: (a) the whole preprocessing recipe - 250 Hz, 3 Hz high-pass, 100 Hz low-pass, 1024-sample (~4 s) windows, per-window min-max normalisation followed by mean subtraction; (b) the synthetic-train / real-test protocol using FECGSYN for training and PCDB plus ADFECGDB for evaluation, which gives a genuinely leakage-free number and which we should adopt verbatim so our results are comparable to theirs; (c) the SINR-stratified training corpus (three tiers at 20,000 / 25,000 / 10,000 segments with the stated SINR_f and SINR_m dB ranges) as a principled way to balance easy and hard cases without a weighted loss; (d) the evaluation convention - Pan-Tompkins on the output, 50 ms TP tolerance, Se/PPV/F1 - which lets us drop our numbers straight into their Table 4; (e) AveragePooling instead of MaxPooling, LeakyReLU(0.01), BatchNorm and Dropout 0.1 as safe defaults for ECG waveform regression; (f) the two-stage insight...

**I. Research opportunity.** Several clean, publishable gaps, most of them cheap for a student project. (1) THE TDA GAP - the paper invokes Takens' theorem but then does nothing topological with the embedded point cloud; it just convolves over the lag matrix. Nobody has asked whether persistent homology on that same embedding adds information the CNN cannot already extract. Our project can answer exactly that, and this paper hands us the perfect control arm: identical preprocessing, identical windows, identical embedding, identical train/test protocol, with and without the PH/persistence-image stage. That is a textbook ablation and it does not exist in the literature. (2) THE EMBEDDING-PARAMETER GAP - L and tau are set to (8, 1) and (4, 1) with no justification, no mutual-information or false-nearest-neighbours analysis, and no sensitivity study. Their embedding spans only 16-32 ms, far shorter than a QRS complex, let alone a cardiac cycle - so the question of whether a genuinely dynamical embedding (tau of tens of milliseconds, L of tens of dimensions, spanning a fetal RR interval) helps is wide open. A systematic L x tau sweep would be an original contribution on its own. (3) THE DETECTOR GAP - this is an extraction network with a hand-crafted Pan-Tompkins bolted on. There is no learned fetal-QRS detection head, no per-sample detection loss, no Gaussian-heatmap or segmentation target, no Dice/focal/weighted-BCE treatment of the extreme positive-class sparsity (roughly 8-10 R-peaks per 4-second window...

**Số liệu then chốt (kèm nguồn trong bài):**

- SCTD-2Net on PCDB: Se 97.19%, PPV 98.80%, F1 97.97% - stated in the Results prose of Section III-C citing Table 4 (PPV 98.8% also appears in the Abstract)
- SCTD-2Net on ADFECGDB: Se 95.17%, PPV 97.94%, F1 96.52% - stated in the Results prose of Section III-C citing Table 4 (PPV 97.94% also in the Abstract)
- SCTD-2Net beats SCTD-1Net in sensitivity by approximately +1.1% on PCDB and +1.56% on ADFECGDB - stated in Results Section III-B referring to Table 2 (the absolute SCTD-1Net values live only inside the Table 2 bitmap, which was not retrievable; DERIVED-BY-SUBTRACTION they would be Se ~96.09% on PCDB and ~93.61% on...
- SCTD-1Net complexity: 7.68 million parameters, 92.78 MB storage, 3.91 GFLOPs per inference - stated in Results Section III-B prose citing Table 3
- SCTD-2Net complexity: 9.22 million parameters, 111.79 MB storage, 4.63 GFLOPs per inference - stated in Results Section III-B prose citing Table 3
- SCTD-Net exceeds AECG-DecompNet by +3.88% Se, +1.58% PPV and +2.74% F1 - stated in the Discussion (Section IV) citing Table 4 (DERIVED, my arithmetic against the PCDB row: AECG-DecompNet would be Se 93.31%, PPV 97.22%, F1 95.23% - not directly reported)
- SCTD-2Net exceeds the 3-4 channel FastICA-AEFLN-NWT by +1.77% F1 on PCDB, and did so over 80 recordings versus that method's 16 - stated in Results Section III-C
- SCTD-2Net achieves +0.56% higher PPV than Improved CycleGAN and +1.06% higher PPV than Attention R2W-Net on PCDB, while being slightly lower on Se and F1 - stated in Results Section III-C (DERIVED: implies PPV 98.24% and 97.74% for those two methods)
- Comparison scope: 7 traditional signal-processing methods and 11 deep-learning methods, 18 in total - stated at the start of Results Section III-C
- Training corpus: 55,000 synthetic segments, split 20,000 high-quality + 25,000 medium-quality + 10,000 low-quality - stated in Methodology Section II-E
- Training SINR strata: high quality SINR_f in [-5, 0] dB with SINR_m in [-5, 5] dB; medium SINR_f in [-15, -5] with SINR_m in [0, 10]; low SINR_f in [-25, -15] with SINR_m in [10, 15] - stated in Methodology Section II-E, SINR defined by Eq. 6
- Preprocessing: downsample to 250 Hz, high-pass cut-off 3 Hz, low-pass cut-off 100 Hz - stated in Methodology Section II-A
